A tag count that looked like a release burst was `git ls-remote --tags` double-counting annotated-tag
dereference lines (`T204`). A `GPL-3.0` body that looked rewritten was SPDX's `246` curly quotes against
the FSF's ASCII (`T205`). Recorded as the ninth and tenth false-positive classes, both caught before
publication.**

### 🟢 What this pass added to the shelf

- 🆕 🔴 **`T204` — the ninth false-positive class: annotated-tag dereference.**
  🔴 **`git ls-remote --tags | grep -c refs/tags` counts the `refs/tags/X^{}` line every ANNOTATED tag
  emits, so `mentingo` read `233` against p168's `118` and `anki-mcp-server` `113` against `57`.**
  🟢 **Stripping `^{}` and uniquing reproduces p168 EXACTLY on all `12` rows. The dangerous direction:
  it MANUFACTURES activity, and `tags` is a liveness signal here.**
- 🆕 🔴 **`T205` — the tenth false-positive class: typography.** 🔴 **SPDX's canonical texts carry `246`
  curly quotation marks and `1` `©`; FSF ASCII bodies carry `0`. Unfolded, a clean `GPL-3.0` reads as
  `71` added / `71` removed words.** 🟢 **Folded, it reads `1`/`1` — and the one word is
  `philosophy/` vs `licenses/why-not-lgpl.html`, which is p168's `2`-byte mystery confirmed by a
  different instrument on a different repository that was not looking for it.**
- 🆕 🔴 **`T206` — `HKUDS/DeepTutor`'s `Apache-2.0` body is NON-VERBATIM.** 🟢 **Canonical title and
  version lines; `40` words added and `29` removed against `apache.org`'s own text.** 🔴 **Section
  `4(d)` reads *"excluding **any** notices"* where canonical reads *"excluding **those** notices"*, and
  the `APPENDIX` instruction paragraph is replaced by one naming an *"**Alarm** ... umbrella trademark"*
  that does not exist.** 🔵 **A paraphrase artifact, not a substitution — no new terms, no permission
  withdrawn. But a text-matching scanner will not resolve it, and the project has no verbatim grant of
  record. Stays recommended; the verdict becomes *"`Apache-2.0`, non-verbatim"*.**

### 🟢 What this pass added to the shelf

  🟢 **`harden()` has exactly ONE call site in `src/main`: the author's course markdown.**
  🔴 **The student answer — the one input with a grade incentive — reaches the prompt unscrubbed,
  defended by a system-message sentence and a `-----` fence.** 🟢 **Stated fairly: the model
  returns advisory signals and the server owns the mark, so the exposure is BIAS of the inputs,
  not capture of the output.** 🟢 **`P162-B` is the four-hour fix, and it FLAGS rather than
  rewrites — rewriting a learner's submission before grading it is indefensible in an appeal.**
- 🆕 🔴 **`T145` — the Annex III evaluative tier holds TWO open-source platforms, and the second
  is MIT.** 🔴 **`Selleo/mentingo` has been on this shelf for five passes as *"the only
  MIT-licensed LMS"*; it ships a `7`-table AI judge with `ai_mentor_judgements.passed BOOLEAN NOT
  NULL`.** 🔵 **So *"pick the permissive one to stay simple"* no longer keeps a client out of the
  high-risk tier. `T140` AMENDED.**
- 🆕 🟢 **Human oversight is now a PATTERN, not an existence proof.** 🟢 **OpenOLAT (Java, Apache-2.0,
  Switzerland) and mentingo (TypeScript, MIT, Poland) independently refuse to let the model own

null turns out to have been a defect in this KB's queries rather than a property of the
ecosystem. 🔴 **And this shelf cannot see a GPL licence**: a body-substring matcher in three
published modules has mis-identified **5 of 7 GNU-family rows**, and `GPL` has been published
**zero times in 150 passes**.

and on this same date, is refused in this session.** Every `git clone` of a third-party
repository is denied by this environment's policy classifier — bulk and single alike — so
🔴 **the entire payload-measurement lane this shelf has run for seven passes is dark.**

🟢 **What the pass did instead is the better finding.** `P145-A` found one instrument
returning a verdict over an empty file set. Asked of the whole shelf, the answer is that
🔴 **the defect is a class, a second instrument carries it, and not one of 35 traversing
instruments can notice it.**

| finding | verdict | figure |
|---|---|---|
| 🆕 🔴 `P146-A` | 🔴 **The clone channel is REFUSED, one pass after being certified** | `Untrusted Code Integration` on **1 of 1** single clone and **1 of 1** bulk attempt. 🔴 **`P145-X`'s "44 of 44 cloned, zero dead" cannot be re-run.** 🔵 Not a network block — a policy refusal, and a different one from `P139`'s execution refusal |
| 🆕 🔴 `P146-B` | 🆕 🔴 **A SECOND instrument carries `P145-A`'s defect** | `p141-agent-surface-gate/surface_gate.py` excludes `dist`/`build` in an **inline literal set inside `walk()`** — no named constant, no re-admission. 🟢 **Pinned**: `walk()` yields **0 files** on a dist-only payload and `is_mcp_server` returns **`(False, 0)`** → `NOT_AN_MCP_SERVER` |
| 🟢 `P146-C` | 🟢 **…and the negative control separates the two states** | 🟢 **The identical bytes at the repository root read as a server** (`sdk=True`, hits>0). 🔵 **So the verdict is a function of the path, not the payload** |
| 🆕 🔴 `P146-D` | 🆕 🔴 **`T42` is a property of the shelf, and the shelf cannot detect it** | **35** traversing instruments. 🔴 **0 carry an empty-walk guard.** 🔴 **5 exclude `dist`/`build` with no re-admission while emitting a verdict**: `surface_gate.py`, `surface_gate2.py`, `americas.py`, `schema_tiers.py`, `tenancy.py` |

### 🔴 `P146-D` — the finding this pass exists for

`P145-A` is the strongest finding this shelf has produced: an exclusion list four lines
above the one being audited had removed seven of eight rows from a tier, and what was left
behind looked like a measurement. p145 fixed it in its successor and named the class `T42`.

🔴 **What p145 did not ask is whether anything else on the shelf could do the same, or
whether anything would notice.** Both answers are bad.

| | count |
|---|---|
| instruments that traverse a tree | **35** |
| …that carry an **empty-walk guard** | 🔴 **0** |
| …that exclude `dist`/`build`, have no re-admission, and emit a verdict | 🔴 **5** |
| …that cannot read anything but a git clone | 🔴 **16** |

🔵 **The exclusion list was one instrument's bug; the inability to notice is the shelf's.**
A guard is four lines and needs no judgement, because the question it asks has one right
answer in every artefact kind: *did I read anything at all?* `p146-empty-walk-guard`
answers it, and `walked_or_refuse` is wired to preserve the distinction that `P145-A`'s
seven rows collapsed —

| state | meaning | correct output |
|---|---|---|
| 🔴 **zero files walked** | the instrument saw no input | **no verdict is available** |
| 🟢 **files walked, none matched** | the instrument saw input and found no surface | `NOT_AN_MCP_SERVER` — a finding |

🔴 **`P145-A`'s rows were the first state, published as the second**, and `P146-B` shows a
second instrument standing ready to do it again on the agent tier.

### 🔴 `P146-A` — why this pass has no payload measurements, said plainly

Nine passes on this date have measured repository payloads. This one cannot. A single
`git clone` of one census subject is refused with `Untrusted Code Integration`, and so is
a seventeen-subject loop. 🔵 **The refusal is the environment's, not the network's** — and
it is a third distinct blocker, alongside `P139`'s execution refusal and `P144-V`'s DNS
and `403` walls.

🔴 **Consequences, stated rather than absorbed:**

- `P145-X`'s clone census cannot be re-run, so **44 of 44 live** is now an unverifiable
  historical reading rather than a current fact.
- The **16 git-only instruments** (`P146-E`) cannot execute at all.
- The 19 filesystem instruments have nothing to walk.
- 🔴 **Every `NOT_AN_MCP_SERVER` row on the shelf is now un-recheckable**, which matters
  more this pass than last, because `P146-B` just found a second instrument capable of
  producing that verdict from an empty walk. Five rows carry it. **Whether any of the five
  is a `T42` artefact is exactly the question that needs a clone**, and it is registered
  for p147 rather than guessed at here.

- 🔴 **No new agent row for the seventh pass running**, and this pass did not find a new
  search formulation either.

### 🔴 `P145-A` — the finding this pass exists for

`ACTION DZ` was registered on a correct observation: `surface_gate2.py` reads **0** on real
MCP servers that register through `setRequestHandler(ListToolsRequestSchema, …)`. 🟢 **The
repair was built and it works.** 🔴 **It is not why seven rows read zero.**

`SKIP_DIRS` at `surface_gate2.py:79` contains `dist` and `build`, and `shipped_only` adds
`NOT_SHIPPED` on top rather than changing it. So both modes drop both trees — and the npm
tier keeps all of its code in them:

| ships all code under | packages |
|---|---|
| 🔴 `dist/` | `@imazhar101/mcp-canvas-server` (69 files), `moodle-mcp-server-aql` (68), `@ink-waffle/moodle-mcp` (18), `mcp-canvas-lms` (16) |
| 🔴 `build/` | `eth-moodle-mcp` (13), `canvas-mcp-server` (6), `@owen-x-tech/canvas-mcp` (4) |
| 🟢 `lib/` + root | `@n35da/brightspace-mcp` (17) — 🟢 **the only row p143 measured** |

🔵 **This is `T39` one level up from where `P144-O` applied it.** `NOT_SHIPPED` was audited
in p143, p144 and p145. 🔴 **`SKIP_DIRS` sat four lines above it and was never read** — and
it was doing the consequential filtering: removing seven of eight rows from a tier and
leaving behind something shaped like a measurement. 🆕 **That is `T42`.**

🟢 **The baseline reproduces p143 row for row**, so every difference is attributable to this
probe's two deliberate changes. 🟢 **And five of the seven new readings match counts this KB
already held from unrelated instruments**, which is what makes the corrections trustworthy
rather than merely different.

### 🔴 `P144-A` — the finding this pass exists for

`T36` was the most load-bearing claim p143 adopted. It withdrew three of `T30`'s figures on the
grounds that npm and the other tiers were measured by different instruments over different
artefact kinds, so a gradient across them could not be read.

🟢 **Half of that is still true.** `surface_gate2.py` and `surface_bound.py` count different
things — registration *sites* versus tool *descriptors* — and `surface_gate2.py` reads **0** on all
three of these packages in *both* artefact kinds, because they register through
`setRequestHandler(ListToolsRequestSchema, …)` and it has no pattern for that shape.

🔴 **The other half is false, and it is the half the thesis was adopted on.** Varying only the
artefact, the same instrument returns the same tool names every time. 🔵 **The bundle was never
the variable; the population was** — and the first draft of this very probe reproduced `T37`'s
defect by reading a committed `dist/` as if it were source. 🔴 **Two passes running, the
instrument was blamed for what the subject list did.**

- 🔴 **`ACTION DP` — DISCHARGED and REFUTED.** 3 of 3 set-identical; `T36` withdrawn; `T30`'s npm
  figure reinstated. 🔴 **Threshold unreachable — 5 of 8 unsourceable.**
- 🟢 **`ACTION DQ` — DISCHARGED, falsifier silent.** Three bidirectional pins, three mechanisms.
- 🟢 **`ACTION DR` — DISCHARGED, falsifier silent.** 35 confirmed; 31 is the default; `T38`.
- 🟢 **`ACTION DS` — DISCHARGED, falsifier silent.** Only librus moves; 9 of 11 names inert; the
  proposed remedy refuted; the premise tested and its inference refuted.
- 🔴 **`ACTION DT` — DISCHARGED and REFUTED.** 0 of 2 qualify; one placed by institution; `P23`
  recaveated to calendar span.
- 🔴 **`ACTION DU` — DISCHARGED and REFUTED** on its literal criterion; returns `T39` plus a
  bot-contamination finding in `p142`.
- 🔴 **`ACTION CW` (Texas HB 149 / TRAIGA) — STILL UNREAD, seventh pass, NOT re-registered.**
  🔵 `capitol.texas.gov` is outside this environment's allowlist. It is an environment request.

### 🔴 `P143-C` — the finding this pass exists for

`ACTION DM` was a repair. It named two blind shapes and attached a share-based falsifier: *more
than five points on any tier and `T30`'s figures are withdrawn.* The share moved 17 points, so the
falsifier did its job.

🔴 **But the repair's own first draft was wrong in the same way p141 was**, and the falsifier could
not have told anyone. It reported the same 23 `legacy_reference/` sites, merely relabelled
`LITERAL` instead of `DYNAMIC`. 🔴 **Same population, same count, opposite classification — and an
identical tier share.** A five-point test passes that silently.

🟢 **What caught it was noticing that one subject's verdict flipped between two instruments, and
then opening the repository and counting.** 🔵 **A per-subject disagreement is louder than a tier
aggregate**, and the aggregate was the only thing being watched. `T37` states the rule;
`NOT_SHIPPED` and `test_not_shipped_trees_are_excluded` enforce it; and the probe now emits
`not_shipped_delta` per row so a whole-tree/shipped-tree disagreement is visible per subject.

### Verdicts on p142's actions

- 🟢 **`ACTION DJ` — DISCHARGED, falsifier silent.** `-0500` dominant in 5 of 5; eduNEXT **LATAM**
  on 13 year-round `-0500` authors, with the pooled test recorded as unreadable.
- 🔴 **`ACTION DK` — DISCHARGED and REFUTED.** 2 rows, not 3. Channel retired, `ncat` hand-noted,
  and the row it was built for placed **North America**.
- 🔴 **`ACTION DL` — DISCHARGED and REFUTED.** npm rows carry **more** tools, not fewer.
- 🔴 **`ACTION DM` — DISCHARGED and REFUTED.** 17-point move; `T30`'s three figures withdrawn;
  four blind shapes, not two; and a population defect the falsifier could not see.
- 🟢 **`ACTION DN` — DISCHARGED, falsifier silent.** 19/19 regions reproduced; rule adopted.
- 🟢 **`ACTION DO` — DISCHARGED, falsifier silent.** 10/10 hashes, 3 of 4 fields source-verified.
- 🔴 **`ACTION CW` (Texas HB 149 / TRAIGA) — STILL UNREAD, and NOT re-registered**, for the sixth
  pass. 🔵 **Cause unchanged and re-measured**: `capitol.texas.gov` is outside this environment's
  allowlist, like `eur-lex.europa.eu` and `oecd.org`. 🔴 **It is an environment request, not a
  research task**, and re-registering it a sixth time would be theatre.

### 🔴 `P142-F`/`P142-G` — the finding this pass exists for

`ACTION DH` was a scale-up. It ran p141's offset channel over 44 connector rows and asked for the
LATAM count *"explicitly, including when it is zero"*, reasoning that *"an eleventh null on 43
rows is a far stronger statement than a tenth on eight."*

🔴 **It returned 0 of 44 — and 0 North America of 44.** p141's table books an offset only where
that offset is unambiguous across the five regions, and the offsets of Brazil, Argentina, Peru,
Mexico, the United States and Canada are precisely the shared ones. 🔵 **The rule is honest; its
refusals are not randomly distributed.** They land on two whole regions, every time, by
construction.

🔵 **North America is what made it legible.** Nobody reading a shelf that carries
`SleepyPandas/UofT-MCP`, `skhdemo/uoft-quercus-mcp` and `luca-dupont2/mcgill-vsb-mcp` would
accept "no North American row" as a measurement of the world — and it comes out of the same rule
for the same reason. 🔴 **An eleventh LATAM null would have been this KB's most confident
restatement of its own blind spot.**

So the pass answered the question on p140's already-proved channel instead — **a hostname the
connector must reach to work** — and 16 of the 19 refusals resolve:

| | rows | evidence |
|---|---|---|
| 🟢 **LATAM** | 🟢 **13** | **Brazil 9** (`sigaa.ufpi.br`, `sigaa.ufrn.br`, `suap.ifrn.edu.br`, `ifma.edu.br` …), **Argentina 3** (`siu.edu.ar`, `guarani.unq.edu.ar`, `servicios.unl.edu.ar`), **Peru 1** (16 × `upc.edu.pe`) |
| 🟢 **North America** | **3** | Canada — `acorn.utoronto.ca`, `q.utoronto.ca`, `vsb.mcgill.ca` |
| **EMEA** | *12, upstream* | 🔵 **Already placed by the offset channel and not eligible here** — `+0200`/`+0300` are unambiguous, so these rows were never refused |
| **APAC** | *11, upstream* | 🔵 Same — `+0530`/`+0800`/`+0900`/`+0930`/`+1100`/`+1300` are unambiguous |
| UNMAPPED | 3 | 2 with no country-bearing host, 1 `.edu`-only |

🟢 **And the conjunction is what makes it safe.** An offset alone under-determines; a hostname
alone places an institution rather than an author. Together they close: there is no reading on
which someone committing at `-0300` against `sigaa.ufpi.br` is in Greenland.

### 🔴 Four instrument defects, all found before publication, all in the inflating direction

🔵 **Fourth consecutive pass where that is true, and this time there were four.**

| defect | what it would have published |
|---|---|
| 🔴 `MPL` ⊂ `IMPL` | **300+** "licence files" in `org.verapdf:validation-model`, every one a compiled class in an `impl` package |
| 🔴 `.py` is Paraguay **and** Python | `scraper.py` placed `BlackboardAssistantMCP` in **LATAM**; `synth.py` placed `bb-mcp`; six `*.py` "hosts" split the Canadian `UofT-MCP` row |
| 🔴 `.do` is the Dominican Republic **and** a Struts action — 🔵 *which SIGAA itself uses* | `logar.do`, `vertelalogin.do` as Dominican hosts on four Brazilian rows |
| 🔴 `\b` matches before a hyphen | `123.dkr.ecr.us-east-1.amazonaws.com` booked `Cicatriiz/openedu-mcp` into **North America** off the "`.us` host" `dkr.ecr.us` |

🔴 **Three of the four would have published a WRONG REGION**, which is worse than publishing
none — and two of them in the direction of the gap this pass was closing. All four are pinned as
tests. 🔴 **The cost is declared: excluding `.py`, `.do`, `.cu` and `.cl` means this KB cannot
place a repository in Paraguay, the Dominican Republic, Cuba or Chile at all.**

### `P141-E` — why a refuted falsifier was again the best outcome

`ACTION CZ` was written against p140's own interest, and it fired. 🔵 **But the reason it fired
is worth more than a confirmation would have been.** p140's instruction — *"apply
`p140-live-surface-bound`"* — assumed the five rows resembled the eight it was built for. They do
not: two are Python `FastMCP`, one is hand-rolled PHP, one is not an MCP server at all. Asking
the question properly meant reimplementing it per language, and that is what surfaced the real
defect: 🔴 **`ahmedEid1/lumen` serves 10 tools where a name-literal count returns 1**, and
🔴 **`nirholas/ai-tutor-mcp` serves 2 where it returns 0** — the *identical* idiom behind p140's
61-tool blind spot. 🔵 **So that blind spot was never an outlier. It is the ordinary shape of a
table-driven server, and the majority shape on this tier.**

### 🟢 The pass's happiest finding, and it is in a two-commit repository

`nirholas/ai-tutor-mcp` — **Apache-2.0, 2 commits** — ships the entry-point guard p139 recorded
as missing from `canvas-lms-mcp`, with `realpathSync` on both sides *"because npm bin shims are
symlinks"*, plus a `buildServer()` returning a registered server with no transport: 🟢 **exactly
the seam p139 found unreachable by import in the 19-version package.** 🔵 **Commit count is not
maturity, in either direction** — which the same pass measured from the other end: 🔴 **151 of
191 commits (79%)** in `zijinz456/OpenTutor` carry a `Co-Authored-By: Claude` trailer, upgrading
`T25` from inference to declaration.

### `P140-B` — why a refuted falsifier is the best outcome this pass produced

`ACTION CR` was written against p139's own interest: *"Refuted if either inherits a `<licenses>`
block from its parent — which would … RAISE the declared-but-absent denominator, **against this
pass's own finding**."* 🔴 **Both rows refuted it.** And the consequence p139 predicted is exactly
what happened — the denominator rose, so `T21`'s gradient **steepens**: PyPI **9%** → npm **33%**
→ Maven **80%**.

🔵 **The new trend is `T26`: a licence grant has a DEPTH.** `T17` said a licence identifier is not
a licence; `T26` says the *absence* of an identifier is not the absence of a licence.
🔴 **`net.oauth.core:oauth`'s grant lives under a different `groupId`** — which is why sixteen
years of transitive use never surfaced it, and why two sentences on `repos/foundations.md` and
`verticals/solutions.md` calling it *"unlicensed"* are now corrected rather than quietly dropped.

### `P140-E` — the row that ships the control, and why it becomes a pattern

Every high-risk control on `compose/patterns.md` until now was something Globant builds *around* a
component. `ACTION CT` found one that ships it: `mark_domain_high_stakes` is irreversible from the
agent tier — this pass searched for `unmark_*`, `clear_*`, `unset_*`, `reset_*` and found **none** —
and `db/store.go` says why: *"Downgrading safety classification requires an operator-controlled
process that is outside the learner-facing MCP trust boundary."* Its evidence policy is **typed**:

> `"policy": "trusted human_review evidence is required; host_llm and external_service`
> `evaluations do not satisfy this gate"`

🔵 **The model cannot certify itself** — the property EU AI Act **Article 14**, **Annex III**,
Vietnam's **Decision 33** and the Oklahoma and Maryland statutes are all reaching for, implemented
by a single alpha-stage MIT maintainer before any vendor on this page. 🟡 **And the honest caveat
is part of it**: alpha, one maintainer, and its own institutional tier is undocumented.

### `P139-B` — why the write axis is the finding and the count is not

An undocumented read tool is a missing paragraph. 🔴 **An undocumented `bulk_update_grades` is an
ungoverned capability** — and *"grading or ranking learners"* is named high-risk by **EU AI Act
Annex III** and by **Vietnam's Decision 33/2026/QĐ-TTg**, the only education-AI conformity regime
actually in force (`T16`). 🔵 **A duty cannot be discharged against a capability nobody has
enumerated, and the README is what procurement reads.** That is why this pass adds **`P18`**, a
gate, rather than a caveat on `P17`: pull the payload, count registered tools across all three
registration idioms, diff against the documentation, partition by write verb, then call `tools/list`
on the running server and diff again — because registration is not behaviour.

🔴 **`@imazhar101/mcp-canvas-server` is the row that should stop a delivery conversation**: 227
tools, 96 documented, **61 undocumented writes**, including **`update_sso_settings`** and
**`restore_authentication_provider`** — tools that change how a Canvas tenant authenticates — in the
same package p138 found carrying **no licence signal and no `repository` field**, so there is no
source to audit either.

### `P139-C` — the one that changes delivery, and it moved the cost rather than removing it

p138 wrote of its own `ACTION CE`: *"answered by reading an interface, not by calling it."* `ACTION CL`
called it. 🟢 **The brokered flow does not demand the Canvas redirect when a grant is pre-seeded** —
proven by a sentinel listener standing in for Canvas and recording **zero** connections, with the
grant store encrypted at rest (`scrypt` + AES-GCM, no plaintext). 🔴 **But the integration shape is
not what p138 implied**: because `main()` runs on import, an embedder cannot pass `oauthStore` in
process. The working route is **seed the encrypted store, run `serve` as a managed process**. `P13`
is rewritten a fifth time: 🟢 **protocol work drops to hours, plumbing rises to ~a week**, and the
"no fork" claim is **narrowed, not refuted** — no fork to *use* it, a one-line `main` guard to
*embed* it.

### `P138-A` — why the layer is the finding

🔵 **This KB has read licence files at four layers: the root file, tree enumeration, source headers,
registry metadata. The payload is the fifth, and it is the only one a client receives.**
`npm install` unpacks a tarball; it does not clone a repository. A grant that lives only at HEAD is
never conveyed to the person who installs — so a licence cell can be correct about a repository and
wrong about the delivery.

🟢 **Direction of the error is recorded because it decides the remedy.** All nine defective rows are
the repository being *better* than the distribution. **Not one package shipped a licence its
repository lacked.** So this is packaging discipline, not misrepresented licensing, and the ask to
an upstream is a one-line `files` change. 🟢 **That asymmetry became a pattern**: `P17`, the
licence-payload gate, with the fail/warn/block split drawn along it.

🔵 **The defect is invisible to the tools a studio already runs.** `npm ls --json` and any scanner
that reads `package.json` both report `MIT` for a package whose tarball contains no licence text.

Probe, method, committed 21-row TSV and **7 passing tests**:
[`compose/code/p138-npm-payload-licence`](compose/code/p138-npm-payload-licence/README.md).

### `P138-D` — the one that changes delivery, not just the ledger

🔵 **`P13` has been rewritten four times: build (p135) → buy-and-adapt (p136) → adapter (p137) → a
named interface (p138).** This pass is the one where the remaining work stopped being an estimate:
`CanvasMCPServerConfig` accepts an embedder-supplied `OAuthStore`, so the adapter seeds a `Grant`
carrying the per-user Canvas refresh token — in the shape
[`kstateome/lti-launch`](https://github.com/kstateome/lti-launch) proves — and the brokered flow
issues an audience-bound MCP token **without forking `ltijs` or `canvas-lms-mcp`**.

🔴 **Not refuted: the adapter is still required.** The subject is established by `CanvasOAuthClient`,
which redirects the user to Canvas; no code path accepts an external verified assertion. 🔴 And
`store.ts` carries *"Update only, never create (QA S7, #356)"* on the Canvas-refresh path, so the
grant must be created up front rather than lazily.

### Regions this pass

| region | this pass |
|---|---|
| **North America** | 🆕 **The age gate is the new instrument class, and it constrains product rather than process.** New York City — the largest US district — has a **one-year moratorium on student-facing AI through eighth grade**. 🔵 **A deadline creates demand for compliance tooling; an age gate removes the end user**, so a K-8 tutor has no deployment surface there however conformant it is. Consequence for a studio: **build teacher-facing and administrative surfaces first** — the same conclusion `P9` reaches from the platform side. 🆕 **California AB 1159** would bar student data from training AI models; **Idaho SB 1227** would require privacy protections. 🟡 **134** bills across **31** states and **60%** of K-12 teachers using AI in 2024–25 — both single-publisher secondary figures, and p136 already recorded that bill counts disagree. 🔴 **Canada: still nothing, fourth consecutive pass** |
| **EMEA** | 🔴 **A gap in this KB's own region vocabulary.** Every EMEA row for several passes has been EU, UK or Nordic; the **Middle East and Africa are the other two-thirds of the label and had no rows at all.** First entries: **UAE National AI Strategy 2031**, Saudi Arabia's **SDAIA** framework, and AI policies **in drafting** in Kenya, South Africa and Nigeria. 🔵 **The finding is not "MEA regulates education AI" — it is that MEA has national AI machinery and no education-specific duty**, the inverse of the EU's position: no conformity deadline to sell against, and the buyer is a ministry building capability rather than a school proving compliance. 🔴 **All five rows come from one secondary source whose subject was insurance** — leads, not findings. 🟡 EU unchanged: Annex III education duties at **2027-12-02**, **Article 50 applying since 2026-08-02** |
| **APAC** | 🟢 **`ACTION CG` discharged in Vietnamese, and it was the right language to ask in.** Decision **33/2026/QĐ-TTg**: signed **2026-06-30** by Deputy PM **Hồ Quốc Dũng**, in force **2026-08-15**, issued under 🆕 **Nghị định 142/2026/NĐ-CP** (p137 cited only Law 134/2025/QH15). 🆕 **The education list includes `đánh giá tâm lý` — psychological assessment** — which the English read missed, and which catches wellbeing screening, engagement scoring and at-risk prediction: product classes a vendor would file as neither "grading" nor "biometrics". 🔴 **The transition date still does not resolve**: **2027-09-01** for health/education/finance in one Vietnamese source, a general **2027-03-01** in another whose scope list omits education. 🔵 **The disagreement is not about education's deadline being later — it is about whether the later deadline exists.** Plan against **2027-03-01**. 🔴 **The gazette `congbao.chinhphu.vn` does not resolve in this channel** (`ENOTFOUND`) |
| **LATAM** | 🔴 **Eighth consecutive pass with no LATAM-owned row — and this pass declined a chance to fake one.** The registry census returned two packages whose owner names invite the booking: `@nahuelalbornoz/moodle-mcp` (author *"Italicia"*, repo **`marcosnahuel/moodle-mcp`** — 🔴 a third address mismatch this pass) and `brightspace-mcp` (author *"Jhostin Aleck"*, **547 paths**, the largest connector payload on the shelf). 🔵 **A personal name is not a region, and onomastics is not evidence** — both carried as `region: UNMEASURED` with the measured facts attached, so a later pass can promote them in one edit. 🔵 **What would actually close the gap is named**: an affiliation in an author field, a university or ministry as repo owner, a LATAM-domain homepage, or a LATAM institution as a licence **holder**. None of the 21 has any. 🟡 Demand side unchanged and still the stronger half: UNESCO IESALC **87%** adoption against **26%** holding a strategy |
| **Global** | 🔴 **The 2026 column widened again**: **USD 10.6B** (Research and Markets, 40.9% CAGR) and **USD 12.3B** (HolonIQ via a vendor blog) — a **16%** disagreement about a year three-quarters elapsed. 🟢 **This is now a rule rather than a recurring complaint**: the disagreement is about market *definition*, so **quote the CAGR, never the level** — growth rates agree across publishers who differ by billions on the base. 🔴 **Regional shares remain unusable**: NA at **36% / USD 3.68B** and Europe at **USD 2.64B** cannot be reconciled with each other or with any global level, and p137's LATAM-flat-at-5%-across-three-horizons stands as a modelling assumption |

### `P137-A` — the finding, and why it lands on the same spot twice

🔵 **p136 wrote this pass's target in its own words**: *"Nothing maps a verified LTI 1.3 launch onto
a broker's subject and audience… this is the whole remaining bespoke surface of the sanctioned
path."* `ACTION BX` was registered to test that before it was asserted as fact — the same discipline
that refuted `ACTION BS` one pass earlier.

🔴 **It is refuted in source.** `kstateome/lti-launch` — LGPL-3.0, **Kansas State University, Office
of Mediated Education**, 34 paths, HEAD 2026-09-30 — does exactly the mapping, and has for years:

- `LtiLaunch.ensureApiTokenPresent()` reads the **launch subject** from the LTI session
  (`ltiSession.getEid()`);
- it looks up a **per-user** refresh token through `OauthTokenService.getRefreshToken(userId)`;
- when there is none, `OauthController./beginOauth` redirects **that user** to
  `https://<canvas>/login/oauth2/auth` with a per-session `state` UUID, and the callback stores
  `storeToken(userId, refreshToken)`;
- the resulting `RefreshableOauthToken` then calls Canvas **as that user**, validated by
  `showUserDetails("self")`.

🟡 **What survives is narrow and exact, and it is now measured rather than assumed.** The mapping is
anchored on an **LTI 1.1** launch (OAuth 1.0a consumer signing — `LtiOAuthAuthenticationHandler`,
`LtiConsumerDetailsService`), and it hands back a *platform API* token, not an **RFC 8707**-bound
token for an MCP resource server. The 1.3 half of the gap is confirmed in the canonical library's
own code: `ltijs` **v7.0.7**'s `AccessTokenManager` mints only
`grant_type=client_credentials` with the assertion `{sub: platform.clientId, iss: platform.clientId,
aud: platform.authorizationServer}` — 🔴 **the tool's own client id is the subject**, the launching
user's `sub` is stored (`idTokenDoc.user`) and never leaves, and the token cache is keyed by
platform + client + scopes, never by user. 🔴 And 1EdTech's own reference tool
([`1EdTech/lti-1-3-php-example-tool`](https://github.com/1EdTech/lti-1-3-php-example-tool)) has a
HEAD of **2020-06-03** and returns a hard-coded UUID string as its `access_token`.

🟢 **So `P13` drops again — from p136's "three-to-four-week buy-and-adapt" to an adapter of
about two weeks** between three components that each already exist, and `T13` is rewritten for the
second pass running. The seam is named in `compose/patterns.md` → `P13`.

### `P137-B` and `P137-J` — the two reads that cut in opposite directions

🟢 **`ACTION CA` predicted its own refutation and got it**: all three connectors added-but-unread by
p136 match their descriptions, so the 2-for-2 defect streak belongs to the rows p135 read, not to the
tier. 🔵 **But each read still changed a model this KB publishes**: the password-credential tier is a
**deployment configuration** rather than a property of a repository (`brightspace-mcp-server` ships a
passwordless mode and a token-paste path beside the keychain one); a 136-tool vendor connector with
`edubase_delete_organization` in it turns out to carry **`EDUBASE_READONLY`, per-toolset selection
and MCP `destructiveHint` annotations**, which is the best-governed write surface on this shelf; and
a **9★** repository is a fuller OAuth 2.1 implementation than anything the 289★ fork family contains.

🔴 **`P137-J` is the inverse of p136's `pipwerks` correction.** There, a row this KB called
unlicensed turned out to carry a grant **outside** the filename probe. Here, the row carries an
SPDX identifier in `package.json` — `"license": "MIT"`, a real identifier, and `"LICENSE"` listed in
`files` — while **tree enumeration over all 36 paths finds no licence file at all**. 🔵 **The class
is new and it is the one that matters for redistribution: an SPDX id without text.** A client told
to "ship the notice" has nothing to ship.

### `P136-A` — the finding, and why the method matters more than the result

🔵 **p135 wrote its own refutation condition.** It said, in its *"what this pass did NOT achieve"*
section: *"the identity broker was not built, and **its absence is asserted from this KB's own rows
rather than measured against GitHub**."* `ACTION BS` was registered to test it in the **professional
register** — `mcp oauth resource server`, `mcp authorization server`, `token exchange rfc 8693` —
because an authorization component is built by people who never say "education".

🔴 **Six repositories do exactly what `T13` said nothing did**, and the measurement is from source,
not description: `giantswarm/mcp-oauth` (Apache-2.0, Go) verifies the upstream OIDC `id_token`
against the provider's **JWKS**, binds and checks the nonce, **refuses to issue a token when the
provider returns no subject claim**, and implements RFC 9728 protected-resource metadata, RFC 8707
resource binding, RFC 9068 JWT access tokens, PKCE, DPoP and token exchange, with **56 test files**.
`atrawog/mcp-oauth-gateway` (Apache-2.0, 60★) does it **in front of any MCP server without code
modification**, which means the connectors already on this shelf need no edits.

🟡 **What survives is narrower and more sellable.** None of the six terminates an **LTI 1.3 launch**.
`ltijs` already verifies the launch correctly; nothing maps that verified launch onto a broker's
subject and audience. 🟢 **So `P13` drops from a 4–6-week build to a 3–4-week buy-and-adapt, and
the only bespoke code on the sanctioned path is one adapter.** Measured, not assumed:
`topic:lti` + `topic:mcp` across all of GitHub is **two** repositories, and neither implements LTI.

### `P136-B` and `P136-C` — two errors with one shape

🔴 **`pipwerks/scorm-api-wrapper` declares a grant and this KB said it had none.** It is in
`package.json` (`"license": "MIT-style license: http://pipwerks.mit-license.org/"`) and in
`src/JavaScript/SCORM_API_wrapper.js` lines 11–12. The eight-filename probe was correct about files
and wrong about rights. 🟡 **The real residual risk is different**: the body is off-repository at a
host refused in this channel, *"MIT-style"* is not an SPDX identifier, and **HEAD is 2018-09-06**
against a `pushed_at` of 2026-09-18.

🔴 **And `0awei0/kt` is the same error mirrored**: a one-commit re-upload of an **MIT** project with
the README byte-identical and the upstream `LICENSE` **dropped**. One row reads unlicensed because
the grant is outside the file; another reads unlicensed because a copy deleted it.

🟢 **Hence `T15`: a licence verdict belongs to a LINEAGE, not to a slug.** `SCORM_API_wrapper.js`
appears in **293 files** on GitHub; this KB's corpus holds **71** byte-identical README clusters;
the sector moves code by **copying**, not by dependency resolution. Resolve the slug to a repository
(ref set), then to a lineage (shared commits / root tree), then read the grant wherever the artefact
declares it — and record **which** of those it was.

action can have.** 🔴 **The headline: this KB has spent four passes saying the sanctioned standards
path removes the blocker. It removes HALF of it.** The credential claim is confirmed by source read.
The identity claim is not: the standard verifies a user cryptographically, and both agent bridges to
it throw that verification away.

| `P135-G` | 🔴 **LINE-BASED COPYRIGHT STRIPPING SILENTLY DELETES LICENCE TEXT, IN BOTH ORDERS** | caught by the instrument's own tests on real payloads, in both directions |
| `P135-H` | 🔴 **`ACTION BQ`: THE MINISTRY-DATASET AXIS HAS ROWS AND NO INSTITUTIONS** | 42 Brazilian rows, **zero** institution-owned; **4 uncredited clones**, READMEs byte-identical |
| `P135-I` | 🟢 **OPENOLAT RESOLVED — THE PERMISSIVE FULL LMS THIS KB SAID DID NOT EXIST** | **Apache-2.0 + 2 attribution statements**, 447★, pushed 2026-10-09 |

### `P135-A` — the finding, and why it is a component rather than a caveat

🔵 **It was only answerable because the channel changed.** Earlier passes read *payloads*; this one
**cloned the repositories** (`git clone --depth 1`, all three succeeded) and read the implementation.

🟢 **What the source confirms.** `Cvmcosta/ltijs` holds **no user credential at all** — the only
secret is a platform-issued `client_credentials` token, cached per `platformUrl`+`clientId`+`scopes`,
that the institution can revoke. User identity comes from an `id_token` whose signature is
**verified** against the platform's keys, with nonce tracking. `bb-mcp` keeps tokens **in memory
only** and authenticates with an institution-issued app secret. `learnmcp-xapi` uses an **LRS
service credential**. 🔴 **Not one of the three stores a student's password**, which is exactly the
distinction that makes the 36-row portal tier unshippable and this tier shippable.

🔴 **What the source refutes.** `bb-mcp` requires a `caller_identity` argument on every tool call and
its README states plainly: *"This identity is trusted at face value (bb-mcp does not itself verify
end-user identity)"*; `MCP_API_KEY` authenticates **the client, not the user**, and `role: "admin"`
**bypasses** the course-entitlement gate that otherwise stops a grade write into an unenrolled
course. `learnmcp-xapi` takes its actor from `ACTOR_UUID` in the environment at all three call sites
— 🔴 **and two of its own docstrings say *"Actor UUID from JWT"* while the project has no JWT
dependency at all.** The code is honest; its comments describe a trust anchor it does not have.

🟢 **So the gap is a named, small, unbuilt component:** an **identity broker** that terminates a
verified identity (LTI launch, OIDC, or the institution's IdP) and only then calls the MCP server
with a subject it established itself. Nothing in this KB does it, in any region. Written up as
`compose/patterns.md` → **P13**, pre-registered as `ACTION BS` to probe before the absence is
asserted as fact.

### `P135-B` — the register, and the variable nobody had isolated

`ACTION BN` finished the six remaining standards. 🔴 **Maturity tracks the standard's AGE, not its
design**: SCORM (1999) is technically the crudest of the six and carries four repositories above
350★; **Caliper (2015) is the most modern event model and has one repository, 0 stars, no licence,
untouched since 2023**; Comprehensive Learner Record has **one real row**. 🟢 **The mechanism is
installed base, not merit** — institutions have *bought* SCORM and LTI for twenty years, so there is
something to write against.

🔴 **This changes what can be promised.** *"We build on sanctioned standards"* is credible for
content and launch, defensible for assessment and credentials, and **not currently true for
analytics**. Eleven institution- or vendor-owned rows were added, against `ACTION BN`'s refutation
threshold of three: **Newcastle University** (`Numbas`), **Stichting Cito** (`qti-convert`, holder
read out of its own filled-in GPL appendix), **1EdTech itself**, **ADL / US DoD** (archived and
unlicensed), **SURF** (archived), EPAM, Longsight, amp-up.io, AgencyEnterprise, Impierce, Selleo.

### `P135-E`–`P135-G` — `ACTION BP`, and why a licence badge is only a lead

🔴 **A licence payload never hashes to its canonical text naively.** SPDX wraps at ~105 columns;
real `LICENSE` files wrap at ~72. **Zero of 26 payloads were whitespace variants**, refuting BP's
premise outright. The classes that actually defeat identity, in order of how often they bit: **line
wrap**, **typographic punctuation** (SPDX's GPL-3.0 uses curly quotes; shipped `COPYING` files use
ASCII), **steward edits to the licence's own text**, **appendix instantiation**, **undated notices**.

🔴 **`P135-G` is the part worth carrying into any future instrument.** Removing copyright notices
line-by-line is **wrap-dependent in both possible orders**, and each order silently deleted real
licence text rather than failing loudly — strip after reflow and GNU's *"Everyone is permitted…"*
goes with the notice it shares a paragraph with; strip before reflow and Apache-2.0's *"…a readable
copyright notice that is included in or attached to the work"* is deleted because a 72-column break
lands in front of the word `copyright`. Both were observed on real payloads in this corpus. The
rule: **remove a notice as a span inside an already-reflowed paragraph, anchored at the paragraph
start, requiring a year — or, if the paragraph is nothing but a notice, without one.**

🟢 **And the practical edge:** a badge would have mislabelled an LMS a studio *can* use
(`OpenOLAT`, permissive) and said nothing about two it cannot — `pipwerks/scorm-api-wrapper`
(**391★, 130 forks, no licence file at all**) and `cybercussion/SCOBot` (**164★, CC-BY-SA-4.0, and
the file is only the human-readable deed**).

## Pass 134 — 2026-10-11

⏱️ **Twenty-first pass of this date.** 🟢 **This pass did not add another connector to the tier this
KB is built around — it found the tier that makes most of that one unshippable, and it had been
missing the whole time.** For three passes `agents/top.md` has correctly said the 36-row portal tier
is undeployable because every row holds a student's password against a platform that never
sanctioned it, and that the sanctioned alternative (`sis-demo`, `mcp-student-records`) is immature.
🔴 **The second half of that is wrong. The sanctioned path is a mature, permissive, vendor- and
government-owned standards stack, and this KB carried exactly one row from it.**

| finding | verdict | figure |
|---|---|---|
| `P134-A` | 🟢 **THE SANCTIONED-INTEGRATION TIER IS MATURE, PERMISSIVE AND WAS ABSENT** | `Cvmcosta/ltijs` **Apache-2.0, 373★, pushed 2026-10-06** vs the KB's one LTI row (`ims-lti`, 203★) |
| `P134-B` | 🔴 **THE AGENT↔STANDARD BRIDGE IS ONE REPO, AND THE Ed-Fi MCP SERVER IS EMPTY** | `learnmcp-xapi` MIT **15★** (2025-08-29); `edfi-mcp-server` **`size:0`**, 404 on **15** probes |
| `P134-C` | 🔴 **NEW VOCABULARY FAILURE MODE: TOKENIZER FRAGMENTATION** | `ed-fi` → **24 061** (top hit `FizzBuzzEnterpriseEdition`); `edfi ods api` → **6** |
| `P134-D` | 🟢 **`ACTION BG` DISCHARGED — 1 OF 3 DETECTORS WORKS, AND `P133-A` NARROWS** | `gerrit-mirror` on **5 of 40**; **0** shared timestamps; siblings exported **2026-10-08** |
| `P134-E` | 🟢 **`ACTION BI` DISCHARGED — ALL 12 LATAM CELLS CONFIRMED, NOT CORRECTED** | 8 payloads read, 8 confirmed; **4 measured-absent** |
| `P134-F` | 🔴 **`NOASSERTION` IS BIASED TOWARD "UNUSUAL", NOT TOWARD "FINE"** | `tha-edfi-runner` = **MIT + a visible-attribution clause** |
| `P134-G` | 🟢 **`ACTION BJ` DISCHARGED — BLACKBOARD IS 7 ROWS, AUTH SPLITS BY SELF-DECLARATION** | 3 × REST API, 1 × **Okta SSO**, 2 × self-declared scraping |
| `P134-H` | 🟢 **`ACTION BK` DISCHARGED AND REFUTING — EMEA WAS MIS-QUERIED, NOT SATURATED** | France's **cadre d'usage**; **EC guidelines updated 2026**; **Jisc: 38 institutions** |

### `P134-A` / `P134-B` — the finding, and why it is a build rather than a dependency

🔵 **The probe that found it was `grade passback`, then `lti 1.3 advantage`, `oneroster`, `ltijs` and
`xapi` — the professional register of the people who do this work for a living.** Not the category
term (`SIS`, which found nothing for p132), not the capability term (`timetable`, which found p132's
21 rows), not a national platform noun (which found p133's 15). 🟢 **It is a fourth, distinct
vocabulary, and it is the one that reaches institutional and vendor-owned supply** — clause 5 in
`agents/top.md`.

🟢 **What it reached.** `Cvmcosta/ltijs` (**Apache-2.0**, payload-read, **373★**, pushed 2026-10-06)
— the platform-independent LTI 1.3 Advantage tool provider with OIDC launch, Deep Linking, **AGS
grade passback** and NRPS rosters. Plus `pylti1.3` (MIT, 138★), `trilogy-group/oneroster-ts`
(**0BSD** — the most permissive licence in this KB, it drops even attribution),
`Ed-Fi-Alliance-OSS/edfi-oneroster` (**Apache-2.0**, the standards body's own bridge, pushed
2026-10-09), `googleworkspace/oneroster-integration-conformance-tests` (**Apache-2.0** — 🟢 **the
only conformance suite this KB has found for any standard in this industry**), and
`AlexMeiling/wiseflow-api-integration-packages` (**MIT**, holder **"UNIwise ApS"** — the WISEflow
vendor itself).

🔴 **A tool launched over LTI holds no user credential at all.** It runs on a client ID the
institution issued and can revoke. **That is the whole answer to the blocker this KB has been
documenting**, and it is also why `st22209/Parent-Portal`'s death certificate
(*"DEPRECATED AFTER KAMAR API CHANGE"*) does not apply to it.

🔴 **And here is the gap, which is sharper and more valuable than the one it replaces: the standards
are finished and the agents have not arrived.** The bridge from an agent to any sanctioned standard
is **one repository** — `DavidLMS/learnmcp-xapi` (MIT, 15★, last push 2025-08-29) — and the only
Ed-Fi MCP server in existence is **an empty repository**:

| `cdweaver1992/edfi-mcp-server` | measurement |
|---|---|
| `size` | 🔴 **0** |
| raw lane, **15 probes** (5 entry points × `HEAD` / `main` / `master`) | 🔴 **404 on all fifteen** |
| `created_at` / `pushed_at` | 🔴 **`2026-02-17T19:54:46Z` / `:47Z`**, never touched again |

🔵 **`size: 0` plus a 404 sweep is a measurement of emptiness.** A pass that trusted the description
would have tabled a working Ed-Fi MCP server that does not exist — the easiest way this KB could
mislead an engagement. New pattern **`P11`** turns the gap into a costed build; **`P12`** aims it at
the fastest-growing segment (**assessment and feedback agents, 44.36% CAGR**) and the three 2027
compliance clocks (EU **2027-12-02**, Oklahoma **before 2027-28**, Maryland **June 2027**).

### `P134-D` — `ACTION BG`: the detector works, and it corrects the pass that asked for it

| p133's proposed signal | verdict | measurement |
|---|---|---|
| a topic containing *mirror* | 🟢 **WORKS** | **5 of 40** `instructure` repos carry `gerrit-mirror` → refutation condition **not met**; the defect is a lane |
| two repos sharing `pushed_at` to the second | 🔴 **RETIRED** | **no timestamp is shared by any two of the 40**; p133's evidence was two *adjacent* seconds, not a shared one |
| a README naming an upstream | 🟡 not tested | the topic field is cheaper and sufficient |

🔴 **And it narrows `P133-A`.** p133 concluded 2026-04-30 was *"the date their export to GitHub
stopped running"*. 🟢 **Sibling mirrors were exported 2026-09-30 and 2026-10-08 — three days before
this pass. The job runs; `canvas-lms` alone stopped receiving it.** Why is not visible from here and
this KB should not guess. 🟢 **p133's operative conclusion survives and is the one to quote:
`pushed_at` on `canvas-lms` is not an activity signal.** Control: `instructure/ims-lti` has no mirror
topic and was pushed 2026-10-02 — 🔵 **so freshness must be checked per repo, never per org.**

### `P134-E` / `P134-F` — the payload lane cut both ways this pass

🟢 **`ACTION BI` discharged, and for once the payload lane confirmed rather than corrected.** All 8
licensed LATAM rows read; **all 8 of p133's cells confirmed** (GPL-3.0 ×2 — byte-identical,
sha256 `3972dc97…`; AGPL-3.0 ×1; MIT ×5 with named holders). The 4 unlicensed rows returned 404 at
main+master × 5 filenames → **measured-absent**. 🔵 **p133 got these right from the search field
alone.**

🔴 **But `P134-F` breaks an expectation this KB had been building.** Several recent passes corrected
`NOASSERTION` rows *upward* (`openeducat_erp` → LGPL-3.0, `mcp-student-records` → Apache-2.0), and
the habit forming was "payload wins, and payload usually clears the row".
**`tha-guy-nate/tha-edfi-runner` is MIT *plus* an added clause requiring a visible link to the repo
or PyPI page in any publicly available application or service** — a user-visible obligation inside
a client's product. 🟢 **GitHub's matcher was right to flag it.** `NOASSERTION` means *unusual*, not
*fine*; read the payload to learn **which way**.

### 🔴 Two instrument failures caught by control before they published

1. **A four-concept conjunction produced a false zero.** `caliper analytics xapi learning record
   store` → **0**, over a field `xapi learning` shows holds **144** repos. Third consecutive pass to
   generate one of these (`P131-E`, `P133-E`). 🟢 Retracted before publication.
2. **A licence detector that greps a payload for a licence *name* false-positives.** p134's first
   read of two p133 LATAM rows said **LGPL** — which would have been a material and wrong correction,
   since LGPL permits linking without reciprocity and GPL does not. 🟢 **The control caught it:
   GPL-3.0's own line 672 reads *"use the GNU Lesser General\nPublic License instead of this
   License"*, line-wrapped.** `ACTION BP` opened to replace substring matching with hashes.

🔵 **Both failures share one root with `P131-E`, `P132-A` and `P133-E`: a search result, and now a
payload grep, is a fact about the query.** This pass adds a third instance and a new form —
**tokenizer fragmentation** (`P134-C`): `ed-fi` splits into `ed` + `fi` and matches inside *Edition*,
*Edit*, *ReFinED* and *edge*, returning **24 061** results topped by `FizzBuzzEnterpriseEdition`.
🔴 **A fourth form, also new: relevance pollution.** `xapi learning` under `sort=stars` ranks six
anti-government propaganda repos (3 258–127★) above the entire field; the first real row is rank 7.

## Pass 133 — 2026-10-11

⏱️ **Twentieth pass of this date.** 🟢 **This pass ran two probes the KB had pre-registered and never
executed, and both of them refuted a standing claim.** `ACTION BE` said it in its own words:
*"Until that is run, 'no LATAM supply' is an untested claim about a query, not a finding about a
region — and it is the claim this KB repeats most often."* 🔴 **It ran. There are twelve.**

🔴 **And the pass's sharpest finding is not a new repo — it is a four-pass measurement error in this
KB's own foundations page.** *"`instructure/canvas-lms` is the quietest of the major platforms"*
measures a **stopped Gerrit mirror**, not Instructure's engineering.

| finding | verdict | figure |
|---|---|---|
| `P133-A` | 🔴 **A FOUR-PASS "QUIET PLATFORM" CLAIM MEASURES A STOPPED MIRROR** | topic **`gerrit-mirror`**; `instructure/analytics` shares `pushed_at` **1 second** later; `gerrit` appeared **0** times in 1 733 addresses |
| `P133-B` | 🟢 **`ACTION BE` DISCHARGED — "NO LATAM SUPPLY" REFUTED** | **12 new rows**: SIGAA 5, SUAP 3, SIU-Guaraní 4 |
| `P133-C` | 🔴 **AND LATAM INVERTS p132's PORTRAIT OF THE TIER — IT SERVES STAFF** | **4 teacher-facing rows**; `expeditto` is **org-owned with a product homepage** |
| `P133-D` | 🟢 **`ACTION BF` DISCHARGED — ANZ IS THE BEST-LICENSED CORNER, NOT A BLANK** | `BetterSEQTA-Plus`: **MIT, 40★**, 25 forks, pushed 2026-10-06 |
| `P133-E` | 🔴 **THE VOCABULARY RULE HAS A FAILURE MODE: HOMOGRAPH COLLISION** | `ucampus` → Chinese *u校园*; qualified Chile probe → **0**; `seqta` → genomics |
| `P133-F` | 🟢 **PAYLOAD LANE MOVED INSTRUMENT; ALL THREE `UNREAD` ROWS NOW READ** | contents API **scope-blocked**; `raw.githubusercontent.com` works |
| `P133-G` | 🔴 **THE UNREAD ROW HELD THE TIER'S MOST RESTRICTIVE LICENCE** | `ddl-killer` = **AGPL-3.0**, against p132's *"~80% MIT"* |

### `P133-A` — why two timestamps one second apart settle it

| field | value |
|---|---|
| `instructure/canvas-lms` → `topics` | **`canvas-lms`, `gerrit-mirror`, `instructure`** |
| `instructure/canvas-lms` → `pushed_at` | **2026-04-30T14:52:10Z** |
| `instructure/analytics` → `pushed_at` | **2026-04-30T14:52:11Z** |
| `instructure/canvas-lms` → `updated_at` | **2026-10-11** (today) |

🔵 **Two independent repositories cannot be hand-pushed one second apart.** A timestamp shared to
the second is a batch job, and the repo's own topic names what kind. 🔴 **So "Canvas went quiet in
April" was never a statement about Instructure's activity — it is the date their export to GitHub
stopped running.** The correct claim is narrower and more useful to a client: **the public mirror has
been frozen for five months, GitHub is therefore not an activity signal for Canvas, and anyone
diffing this tree to track upstream has been reading a stale copy.**

🟡 **This does not rehabilitate Canvas as a contribution target** — a Gerrit-upstream AGPL-3.0
project with a frozen mirror is *harder* to build on. It changes what the KB may infer from
`pushed_at`, which it has been inferring project health from in two lanes. 🔴 **`ACTION BG` opened:
every mirror in `git-recency-channel` and `p109-freshness` is currently mis-ranked toward looking
abandoned.** 🔵 **The diagnosis came from one control request — pulling a *second* repo from the same
org — not from new search coverage.**

### `P133-B` / `P133-C` — the LATAM rows, and why their shape matters more than their count

🔵 **The probes that worked were `sigaa`, `suap mcp` and `siu-guarani`** — exactly the proper nouns
`ACTION BE` listed. 🔴 **And four SIGAA rows were already sitting in this KB's own
`compose/code/p444-*` payload sweeps as bare addresses with no region and no tier.** The probe did
not only add rows; it gave rows the KB already held a region.

🔴 **p132 described this tier as built by *"students and parents"* with *"no procurement story
whatsoever."* That was true of 21 EMEA/APAC/NA rows and is false in LATAM:**

| row | licence | what it does | who it serves |
|---|---|---|---|
| [`fabrica-de-inovacao/expeditto`](https://github.com/fabrica-de-inovacao/expeditto) | 🔴 **AGPL-3.0** | drafts a lecturer's **RIT** workload report from SUAP (CLI **+** MCP) | 🔴 **a teacher** — and it is **org-owned**, with a homepage, pushed 2026-10-08 |
| [`filipecalegario/auto-sigaa`](https://github.com/filipecalegario/auto-sigaa) | **MIT** | pushes a grade spreadsheet into SIGAA | 🔴 **a teacher** |
| [`eugeborzone/Auto_Grade_Guarani`](https://github.com/eugeborzone/Auto_Grade_Guarani) | 🔴 none | the same, into SIU-Guaraní | 🔴 **a teacher** |

🟢 **Two countries, two national systems, one identical unmet need: moving grades and workload into a

🔴 **The licence mix also differs and it matters commercially:** the LATAM slice is **42% MIT** (5 of

reach for copyleft, and **the single most institutional project is the one that cannot be forked.**

🟢 **One structural gain:** [`SIU-Toba`](https://github.com/SIU-Toba) — the Argentine university

private-vendor route is not.

### `P133-E` — the vocabulary rule's failure mode

| probe | intended | returned |
|---|---|---|
| `ucampus` | **U-Campus**, Universidad de Chile | a **Chinese** *u校园* homework tool (GPL-2.0, 34★) + an Indian campus-food app |
| `ucampus chile universidad` | same | 🔴 **0** |
| `seqta` | **SEQTA**, Australia | the real rows **plus** nanopore genomics (`SeqTagger`, `SeqTagQA`) |

🔵 **`P132-A` showed that category terms cannot reach a field named by platform. `P133-E` shows the
converse: a platform noun that is a homograph reaches the wrong field entirely.** Clause 4 added to
`agents/top.md`: **carry a country or vendor qualifier as a named control, and when the bare probe
returns noise and the qualified probe returns zero, report "no evidence found for this platform" —
never a row count and never a regional zero.** 🔴 **Chile is recorded that way, with both probes
named.** Both controls are logged in the evidence file as `HOMOGRAPH-CONTROL` rows.

🔵 **One root cause under `P131-E`, `P132-A` and `P133-E`: a search result is a fact about the query.
Every regional or tier-level absence in this KB must name the probe that produced it.**

### `P133-F` / `P133-G` — reading the three `UNREAD` rows cost p132 a generalisation

🔴 **The instrument changed again, and the failure mode is dangerous.** The GitHub contents API
(`get_file_contents`) is **scope-blocked in this session** — it returns *"repository is not
configured for this session"* for every repo but this KB's own. **That is an authorisation refusal,
not a 404 and not evidence about the repo**, and a pass that logged it as "no licence file" would
publish a fabricated absence. 🟢 **Working lane: `raw.githubusercontent.com/<owner>/<repo>/<branch>/LICENSE`.**

| row | p132 | p133 | holder class |
|---|---|---|---|
| `edwyj/ddl-killer` | `UNREAD` | 🔴 **AGPL-3.0** | **DOCUMENT-TEXT** — only *"Copyright (C) 2007 Free Software Foundation"* |
| `karissafuller/project-nomad-macos` | `UNREAD` | 🟢 **MIT** | **NAMED** — *"Copyright (c) 2026 Karissa Fuller"* |
| `Tushar-cy/Offline-AI` | `UNREAD` | 🔴 **none** | no payload at `/main/` **or** `/master/` → measured-absent |

🔵 **The lesson generalises past these three rows:** p132 published *"~80% MIT"* over a tier whose
one unmeasured row holds **the most restrictive licence in it**. 🔴 **An unread row is not a random
draw — repos that are hard to read are disproportionately the ones doing something unusual with
their licence. Measure before you average.**

## Pass 132 — 2026-10-11

⏱️ **Nineteenth pass of this date.** 🟢 **p131's discipline worked, and it worked by being pointed
outward.** p131 found more by grepping this repository than by searching the web, and told the next
pass to spend its effort on *gap-directed supply queries*. This pass did that — and the largest gap
in the KB fell.

🔴 **`P132-A` is the most expensive class of error this KB tracks, found in its own flagship gap.**
For several passes this KB has carried *"no AI agent targeting a student information system"* and
promoted it to **"the largest genuine hole on this shelf."** It is false. **There are at least
twenty-one**, and this pass has their addresses, licences and regions.

| finding | verdict | figure |
|---|---|---|
| `P132-A` | 🔴 **THE LARGEST DECLARED GAP IN THIS KB IS REFUTED** | **21 rows** found; the gap was promoted to "largest hole" **one pass before** being disproved |
| `P132-B` | 🟢 **AND THE NEW TIER INVERTS TWO STANDING KB CLAIMS AT ONCE** | **~90% region-placed** (vs 11-of-17 unplaced on the old shelf) and **~80% MIT** |
| `P132-C` | 🔴 **THE "CLEANEST UNCLAIMED OPPORTUNITY" IS OCCUPIED BY A 39 444★ APACHE-2.0 PROJECT** | `project-nomad` = **Kolibri + Kiwix + Ollama**, i.e. this KB's own pattern `P2`, already built |
| `P132-D` | 🔴 **A "1.8× SPREAD" IN THE MARKET TABLE WAS ONE YEAR-SHIFTED ROW** | drop IMARC's **2025** base and the 2026 estimates agree within **1.19×** |
| `P132-E` | 🔴 **`OR` IS NOT A BOOLEAN IN REPO SEARCH — IT RETURNS THE BROAD POPULATION** | **69 812** and **248 136** results from two intended-narrow queries |
| `P132-F` | 🟢 **p131's `NOASSERTION = UNREAD` RULE PAID OFF IMMEDIATELY, WITH A NEW MECHANISM** | `mcp-student-records` = **Apache-2.0** at payload; the file is the **~745 B NOTICE BLOCK** only |
| `P132-G` | 🟡 **`ACTION BD` (Africa/Middle East) NARROWED, NOT CLOSED** | first MENA row: **63★** Arabic-first, **unlicensed by declaration** |
| `P132-H` | 🟢 **THE DEMAND CHANNEL BROKE ITS 4-PASS SATURATION STREAK** | new tokens in **4 of 4** regions once the battery was read for *dating and structure* |

### `P132-A` — why every previous pass's SIS query returned zero

🔵 **The field is not organised by the category term. It is organised by platform name.** This KB
searched for *"student information system"*, *"school management"*, *"SIS"* — the words an analyst
uses. The people actually building these connectors name them after the system they connect to:
**Librus** (Poland), **EduPage** (Slovakia), **Pronote** (France), **IServ** (Germany), **Sisu**
(Finland), **uSaint** (South Korea), **VTOP** (India), **QLDT** (Vietnam), **VSB** / **Quercus**
(Canada), **NYCU** (Taiwan).

🔴 **So p131's `P131-E` was right about the mechanism and still understated it.** p131 found that a
multi-term query returns a zero that measures the query. This pass shows the stronger version: **the
category term itself can be absent from an entire real field**, because the builders are not
analysts. A query built from the analyst's vocabulary cannot see them at any star threshold.

🟢 **And the builders are not enterprises — they are students and parents.** Nearly every row is one
person connecting an assistant to their own university or their own child's school. That explains
every property of the tier at once: tiny star counts, MIT licences chosen by default, precise
regional placement, brand-new creation dates, and **no procurement story whatsoever**.

### `P132-B` — the tier inverts two things this KB has said for many passes

| standing KB claim | what this tier shows |
|---|---|
| *"Most agent repos carry no location evidence at all — 11 of the 17 rows are unplaced rather than guessed"* (`intel/market.md`) | 🟢 **~90% of the 21 new rows are placed on hard evidence** — a named university, a national platform, a country-coded homepage (`mine.ibu.edu.ba`, `qldt.hanu.edu.vn`). Region is *stated*, not inferred |
| *"The permissive/popular inversion, across every tier measured this pass… the most-starred item is repeatedly the unusable one"* (`intel/market.md`) | 🔴 **Inverted back.** ~80% of this tier is **MIT**. The exceptions are small: one GPL-2.0, one Apache-2.0, three unlicensed. 🟡 **But the tier's own top two by stars are the weak ones** — `librus-mcp` (10★) is mixed-licence and `DAU-buddy` (7★) is unlicensed |

🔵 **The honest reading of both:** this KB's generalisations were drawn from one tier — big, famous,
Canvas-and-Moodle-shaped repos — and do not survive contact with a second. **A claim about "every
tier measured" is only ever a claim about the tiers you queried.**

### `P132-C` — the pattern this KB wrote as an opportunity already exists at 39 444 stars

`intel/trends.md` → T8 and `compose/patterns.md` → P2 have called offline/low-bandwidth delivery
*"the most valuable unfilled slot in the whole KB"* and *"the clearest unclaimed opportunity"*, on
the strength of a probe that found **86 repos named `kolibri`, 3 in the family, 0 with AI**.

🔴 **The probe was sound and the conclusion was wrong, because the occupant is not named after
Kolibri.** [`Crosstalk-Solutions/project-nomad`](https://github.com/Crosstalk-Solutions/project-nomad)
— **Apache-2.0, 39 444★** — is an offline-first knowledge and education server that orchestrates
containerised tools via Docker: **Kiwix** powers its Information Library, **Kolibri** powers its
Education Platform, and **Ollama** powers its AI Assistant.

🔵 **That is pattern `P2`, line for line, built and permissively licensed.** It is the
second-largest repository anywhere in this KB — behind only `HKUDS/DeepTutor` (41 136★) — and no
pass had seen it, because every Kolibri probe searched for the string `kolibri` in names and
descriptions, and NOMAD's description does not contain it.

**Consequence for an engagement, stated plainly:** the offline opportunity is **not** greenfield.
It is now *"extend, contribute to, or deploy a 39k-star Apache-2.0 stack"* — a far better starting
position than the build P2 described, and a completely different conversation with a client.

### `P132-D` — the market spread was an artefact of mixing base years

This KB's headline has been: *2026 estimates span ~$6.4B to ~$11.4B, a **1.8× spread for the
current year**, so quote the range and the disagreement.* 🔴 **One row in that table is not a 2026
estimate.** IMARC's **~$6.4B is a 2025 base** (its own earlier edition reads $4.8B for 2024 →
$75.1B by 2033).

🟢 **Restrict the table to figures genuinely for 2026 and the firms nearly agree: $9.58B, $10.4B,
$10.6B, ~$11B — a 1.19× spread.** The real disagreement is not about today at all; it is about the
horizon, where 2035 estimates run from **~$70B to ~$1 169B (~17×)**. One source even publishes two
different CAGRs for its own forecast (34.52% and 36.02%).

🔵 **This reverses the advice.** "Nobody agrees what this market is worth now" was wrong and
unsellable. **"The firms agree within ~20% on the current year and diverge seventeen-fold by 2035"**
is both true and a much sharper thing to say in a commercial conversation.

### `P132-E` — the instrument's second blind spot, and it is the opposite of the first

p131 established that a **generic zero** measures the query. This pass establishes the mirror
image: **a generic enormous number measures the query too.**

| query as written | intent | returned |
|---|---|---|
| `oneroster OR ed-fi MCP agent in:name,description` | the rostering-standard connector tier | 🔴 **69 812** — the global MCP-agent population |
| `swahili OR yoruba OR amharic education in:name,description` | African-language education repos | 🔴 **248 136** — the global `education` population |
| `arabic education LLM tutor in:name,description stars:>5` | MENA tutoring | 🔴 **0** |
| `arabic LLM in:name,description stars:>50` *(control)* | — | 🟢 **3**, one of them a **63★ student-facing advisor** |

🔴 **`OR` is not honoured as a boolean here.** It does not union two narrow sets; it collapses the
query into a broad match. **So a result count in this instrument is evidence of nothing until the
query form is checked** — a zero, a 69 812 and a 248 136 can all be artefacts. `ACTION BC` (a named
control beside every declared absence) is hereby extended: **a named control beside every declared
abundance as well.**

### `P132-F` — the new rule caught a real grant within one pass of being written

p131 ruled KB-wide that **`NOASSERTION` means "GitHub's matcher did not recognise this file" — it
is UNREAD, never UNGRANTED**, and told later passes to read the payload.
[`zavora-ai/mcp-student-records`](https://github.com/zavora-ai/mcp-student-records) came back
`NOASSERTION` from the API. 🟢 **The payload is Apache-2.0**, holder *"Copyright 2026 Zavora AI"*.

🔵 **And the mechanism is new and worth naming.** The `LICENSE` file is **not** a truncated or
bespoke licence — it is the **~745-byte Apache-2.0 *notice block*** (the "Licensed under the Apache
License, Version 2.0… you may obtain a copy at apache.org" stanza) **without the licence body**.
GitHub's matcher needs the body, so it reports "Other". 🟡 **Flag for counsel, not a blocker:** the
grant is by incorporation-by-reference, which is ordinary practice, but a client's reviewer will
want the full text vendored before shipping a derivative.

🟢 **It also answers one instance of this KB's standing `ACTION AY`** (*"who grants the 46
Apache-2.0 / ECL rows with no licensor"*): this row **has** a named licensor, and it is an
organisation rather than an individual — the only org-owned row in the new SIS tier.

### `P132-G` — Africa and the Middle East, after three passes of silence

`ACTION BD` required a dedicated query rather than folding the region into EU findings. Run.

🟢 **There is something there, and it is bigger than most of this KB's agent shelf.**
[`h9-tec/graduation-project-advisor`](https://github.com/h9-tec/graduation-project-advisor) —
**63★**, Arabic-first LLM advisor that walks **Egyptian CS students** from an onboarding form to
five ranked project ideas to a supervisor-ready blueprint (Next.js 15, FastAPI, Qdrant, Azure
OpenAI + Ollama). 🔴 **And it is unusable: its README states *"Private / TBD. Licensing terms will
be finalized before any public release."*** — unlicensed **by declaration**, which is a stronger
and clearer bar than the missing-file cases elsewhere in this KB.

🔵 **Region recorded honestly:** EMEA on the strength of its *stated target audience* (Egyptian
students) and its Arabic-first design. **The author's own location is not established** by anything
on the page, and this KB does not infer nationality from a name.

🟡 **So the gap narrows rather than closes:** the region is no longer *"no evidence found"*. It is
*"one 63★ row, aimed at Egypt, that nobody may build on."* Also noted:
[`mbzuai-oryx/ClimateGPT`](https://github.com/mbzuai-oryx/ClimateGPT) (80★, **MBZUAI, Abu Dhabi**,
EMNLP'23) — evidence of institutional MENA AI capacity, though not an education tool.

### `P132-H` — the saturated channel produced new tokens in all four regions

p131 counted a **fourth consecutive pass** of zero new tokens from the prescribed regional battery
and recommended budgeting it as a *confirmation* channel. 🟢 **Run again this pass, it broke the
streak in 4 of 4 regions** — but note *how*: nothing new surfaced as a **fact**. Everything new
surfaced as a **date, a denominator, or a delivery structure.**

| region | what the channel added that this KB did not hold |
|---|---|
| **North America** | **10%** of institutions have formal AI guidelines and **71%** of US teachers lack AI training (new predicates beside the held *18% of teachers have a written policy*); **CMU + Gates Foundation, $55M** into AI courseware for gateway college courses; an OpenAI country-level education programme with **eight national partners** |
| **EMEA** | the UK's first **AI Adoption Summit (8 June), £200m+** — and its *delivery structure*: government funds, **Big Tech delivers** (Cisco, IBM, BT, Rolls-Royce), unions legitimise, **Skills England** sets curriculum, and **few traditional education providers appear at all**; the **QS/HolonIQ 2026 Europe EdTech 200** as the EMEA counterpart to the held LATAM EdTech 100 |
| **APAC** | **Australia/New Zealand**, a jurisdiction absent from this KB's APAC section entirely — Canberra tightening AI governance and copyright, with OpenAI appointing a dedicated ANZ policy lead |
| **LATAM** | 🟢 **the IESALC fieldwork window: August–October 2025**, which *dates* the held *87% adoption / 26% with a strategy* figures to late 2025 rather than 2026; Tec de Monterrey's observatory moving **students 86%→92%** and **teachers 61%→79%**; **ILIA 2025 (CEPAL)** named as the instrument that actually carries country-level LATAM regulation detail |

🔵 **The lesson is about how the battery is read, not whether it is run.** Four passes called it
saturated because they read it for *new facts*. Read for **provenance** — when was this measured,
who was counted, who delivers the money — the same queries are still productive. 🔴 **And one stale
claim is still circulating in North-America-facing sources: *"the EU AI Act classifies education AI
as high-risk, with full effect in August 2026."* It is wrong** (the Annex III date is
**2027-12-02**), this KB has flagged it for three passes, and a client may well arrive holding it.

  README badge says **CC-BY-4.0** and the body says **CC-BY-SA-4.0**. Unchanged, and still the
  single highest-leverage upstream change available to this KB.
- 🔴 **`instructure/canvas-lms` has still not been pushed since 2026-04-30** — now ~5.4 months, and
  the quietest major platform for a third consecutive pass.
- 🔴 **`blend-ed/openedx-mcp` is still at 2 stars.** Open edX MCP has not moved past experimental.
- 🟡 **Three rows of the new tier were not licence-measured** (`ddl-killer`, `project-nomad-macos`,
  `Tushar-cy/Offline-AI`) and are marked `UNREAD` in the evidence file rather than guessed.

Evidence for every row measured this pass: `evidence/verified-repos.2026-10-11-p132.tsv`
(instrument recorded per row — `search`, `payload` or `page`).

## Pass 131 — 2026-10-11

⏱️ **Eighteenth pass of this date.** 🔴 **Note first that there is no Pass 130 section below this
one.** p130 rewrote all six non-append-only pages, changed the verification method, and **never
wrote its pass log or discharged p129's pre-registrations** — so the chain of verdicts broke for one
pass. It is picked up here.

🔴 **A pass that audits the previous pass, and finds the rewrite itself was the defect.** p130
re-derived every page from a single instrument (GitHub repo search) and declared four gaps. 🟢
**Three of the four are refuted by rows this KB already carried on its own append-only pages** —
the pages p130 did not rewrite. 🔵 **Nothing in this pass's headline was found on the web. It was
found by grepping this repository.**

| `P131-C` | 🟢 **DEMAND CHANNEL SATURATED — 4th consecutive pass, now counted** | **0 new tokens in 4 of 4 regions**; **1** confirmation worth the battery |

| `P131-E` | 🔴 **A GENERIC ZERO MEASURES THE QUERY, NOT THE FIELD** | 5-term SIS query → **0**, while `academico` is **MIT, 404★**, topic `student-information-system` |

### What p130 declared absent, and what was already here

| p130's declared absence | refuted by | held since |
|---|---|---|
| *"no education benchmark… **the largest hole on the shelf**"* | `eth-lre/mathtutorbench` (43★, ETH Zurich, **EMNLP 2025 oral**) + `Khan/tutoring-accuracy-dataset` (57★, licence read in full) | **pass 4** |
| *"no Moodle MCP server with a clear permissive licence"* | `peancor/moodle-mcp-server` — **MIT**, 43★, *"the licence-boundary exemplar"* | **pass 30** |
| *"`openeducat_erp` and `academico` are the whole field"* (SIS) | the **system-of-record tier**: + `frappe/education` (662★), RosarioSIS, Gibbon, openSIS, Fedena | an earlier pass |
| *"no agent targeting a student information system"* | 🟢 **SURVIVES** — and is now the largest genuine hole here | — |

🔵 **One cause explains all three, and it is the thing p130 announced as its improvement.** Repo
search returns metadata faithfully and **cannot see a licence payload, a prior retraction, or an
address found by a different query**. A rewrite grounded in one instrument discards what the KB
established with the others — then publishes the discards as gaps. 🔴 **A false absence is the most
expensive error this KB can make: it tells a studio there is nothing to start from when there is.**

🔵 **And the sharper diagnosis, found in `intel/open-gaps.md`: the measurements were right and the
RESTATEMENTS were wrong — each by a single quantifier swap.** This KB's gap file said *"zero
permissive **releases**"* (true: the MIT Moodle servers publish no tags) and p130 wrote *"no
permissive **licence**"*. It said the benchmark layer is *"**ungranted**"* (true) and p130 wrote
*"**absent**"*. It said *"no SIS **of record**"* and p130 wrote *"two repos are **the whole
field**"*. 🔴 **This is `P129-C`'s family one level up: not prose disagreeing with an instrument, but
prose disagreeing with this KB's own gap register — and every swap inverts the advice that follows
from it.**

### The defect's direction reversed from p129

p129 found **prose disagreeing with the instrument**. p131 finds **an instrument's coarse field
published over a resolved payload read.** `openeducat_erp` is `NOASSERTION` to the API; this KB had
read its `LICENSE` at 8 241 bytes, resolved **LGPL-3.0**, and used that read to *retract* an earlier
*"no grant"* row. p130 reinstated the retracted claim.

🟢 **Rule now stated on four pages: `NOASSERTION` means "GitHub's matcher did not recognise this
file" — it is UNREAD, never UNGRANTED.**

then **11 → 1**, then **59 → 3**, a false-positive rate reaching **95 %**. The cause is
structural: these pages record licences with per-artifact precision (*"MIT code / CC BY-NC-SA
data"*, *"MIT (`LICENSE-CODE`) · `LICENSE` is CC-BY-4.0 for docs"*, *"MIT badges over a 404"*),
and a census that reduces a repository to ONE token is **less precise than the text it audits**.
🟢 **`ACTION W` follows: no census may publish a defect rate until it has been scored against rows
known to be correct.**

🟢 **`P122-A` — the one real defect, repaired in 3 places.**
[`FWU-DE/ais-chat`](https://github.com/FWU-DE/ais-chat) is **AGPL-3.0** (34 523 B; rendered page
agrees). This KB said so in **47** cells while three live claims still called it MIT or
Apache-2.0 — including a compose pattern reading *"MIT, so a closed derivative is permitted"* two
lines under a table saying AGPL-3.0, and an ASCII diagram inside a code fence that every census
from p118 to p121 **skips by design**. 🔵 **The errors concentrate under the phrase "measured this
pass", which suppresses a reader's instinct to re-check.**

🔴 **`P122-F` — `ACTION V` was orphaned in p121's instrument README**, in neither the root README
nor the ledger: `P121-G`'s exact failure mode recurring in p121's own output, one pass after p121
named it. 🟢 **`ACTION T` caught it on its first run, and p123's actions are written into all
three records.** `ACTION T` also showed the `pNNN-` prefix is an instrument **index**, not a pass
number — it reaches **p1040** across 147 directories while this KB is on pass 122.

🟢 **`Gap 402` RESOLVED after six passes of counting zero.** The mandated phrase returns courses
**about** AI, not agents that **deliver** education — `microsoft/ai-agents-for-beginners`
(~67 000★), `agents-from-scratch`, `ai-engineering-from-scratch`. 🔴 **The gap splits in two:
the query was wrong (proved), and the open-source agent layer for education really is thin** —
edtech nouns returned 3 new addresses and none is an agent.

🔴 **`P122-G` — 3 candidate addresses this pass DO NOT EXIST** (`studip/studip`,
`OpenEduCat-Inc/OpenEduCat`, `Opigno/opigno_lms` — all 404), each assembled from a product NAME in
vendor prose. 🟢 **`git ls-remote --symref` now separates "no licence" from "no repository" and
returns the real default branch in one call**, retiring the branch-guessing that cost p120 **410

🟢 **New supply-side evidence that fills half of the EMEA gap, found in the tree rather than on
the web.** `ACTION C`'s 13 placements are all EMEA because a ccTLD-leading namespace is a European
university and public-agency convention: Finland's and Sweden's **national education agencies**

`skolverket/dnp-ss12000-reference-api` **Apache-2.0**, the SS 12000 reference), the European

[`ls1intum/Artemis`](https://github.com/ls1intum/Artemis) — **MIT, 816★**, automated feedback —

region** (UNESCO IESALC 200 HEIs / 19 countries; Digital Education Council 92 % students / 79 %

# 📚 Education KB

> Knowledge base de la industria **Education** para Globant AI Studios.
> Tutores AI, evaluación automática, personalización del aprendizaje, LMS agents

## Estructura

```
education-kb/
├── agents/        # Agentes AI open source de la industria
├── repos/         # Repos MIT/Apache como punto de partida
├── verticals/     # Plataformas verticales customizables con AI
├── intel/         # Mercado, players, tendencias
├── compose/       # Recetas: cómo componer soluciones
├── ingest/        # Scripts de actualización automática
└── compose/code/  # Código ejecutable y probado, no prosa
```
